TRUST LAW
PART 1: GENERAL
1.
Title and repeal
This Law repeals and replaces the Trust Law 2005 (DIFC Law No. 11 of 2005) as it was in force
immediately prior to the commencement of this Law (the "Previous Law"), and may be cited as the
"Trust Law 2018" or "this Law"
2.
Legislative authority
This Law is made by the Ruler.
Application of the Law
3.
This Law applies in the jurisdiction of the DIFC.
Scope of the Law
4.
This Law applies to all DIFC trusts, other than trusts created under the Investment Trust Law (DIFC
Law No. 5 of 2006), and to foreign trusts in respect of all acts, omission or transaction occurring in
the DIFC, whenever the trust was created, provided that all questions arising from acts or omissions
occurring prior to the commencement of this Law shall be determined in accordance with the Previous
Law.
Date of enactment
5.
This Law is enacted on the date specified in the Enactment Notice in respect of this Law.
Commencement
6.
This Law comes into force on the date specified in the Enactment Notice in respect of this Law.
7.
Interpretation
Schedule 1 contains:
(1)
interpretative provisions which apply to this Law; and
(a)
a list of defined terms used in the Law.
(b)
Schedule 2 contains provisions relating to the application of the Arbitration Law.
(2)
Administration of this Law
8.
This Law is administered by the DIFCA.
Default and mandatory rules
9.
Except as otherwise provided in the terms of the trust, this Law governs the duties and
(1)
powers of a trustee, relations among trustees and the rights and interests of a beneficiary.
The terms of a trust prevail over any provision of this Law, except:
(2)
the requirements for creating a trust;
(a)
the duty of a trustee to act in good faith and in accordance with the purposes of the
(b)
trust;
1

10.
Common law and principles of equity
The common law of trusts and principles of equity supplement this Law, except to the extent
(1)
modified by this Law or any other DIFC law or by the Court.
The statute law of England and Wales applicable to trusts does not, except to the extent it is
(2)
replicated in this Law, apply in the DIFC.
This Law does not affect the law applicable to resulting or constructive trusts but the powers
(3)
of the Court under this Law may be exercised in relation to a constructive trust or a resulting
trust.

TRUST LAW
PART 2: CHOICE OF GOVERNING LAW; PLACE OF ADMINISTRATION
11.
Governing law
The meaning and effect of the terms of a trust are:
(1)
determined by the law of the jurisdiction expressed by the terms of the trust as the
(a)
governing law; failing which
to be implied from the terms of the trust; or failing which
(b)
to be determined by the law with which the trust at the time it was created had the
(c)
closest connection.
The references in Article 11(1)(a) and (b) to "failing which" include references to cases:
(2)
where no law is expressed or implied under Article 11(1)(a) or (b); and
(a)
where a law is so expressed or implied, but that law does not provide for trusts or
(b)
the category of trusts concerned.
In ascertaining, for the purpose of Article 11(1)(c), the law with which a trust had the closest
(3)
connection, reference shall be made in particular to:
the place of administration of the trust designated by the settlor;
(a)
the situs of the assets of the trust;
(b)
the place of residence or business of the trustee; and
(c)
the objects of the trust and the places where they are to be fulfilled.
(d)
A settlor may, whether or not he is resident in the DIFC, expressly declare in the trust
(4)
instrument that the laws of the DIFC shall be the governing law of the trust.
A term of the trust expressly declaring that the laws of the DIFC shall govern the trust is
(5)
valid, effective and conclusive regardless of any other circumstance.
12.
Provision for change of governing law
Where a term of a trust so provides, the governing law may be changed to or from the law
(1)
of the DIFC, in the manner prescribed by the terms of the trust, if:
in the case of a change of the governing law to the law of the DIFC by a foreign
(a)
trust, such change is recognised by the governing law previously in effect; or
in the case of a change of the governing law from the law of the DIFC, the new
(b)
governing law would recognise the validity of the trust and either all the trusts,
powers and provisions of the trust remain enforceable, capable of being exercised
and so taking effect or, prior to the change, the trustee obtains the approval of the
Court to the change.
Where the governing law of the trust changes to DIFC law, that trust shall be for all purposes
(2)
a DIFC trust.
(3)
A change in the governing law shall not affect the legality or validity of, or render any person
liable for, anything done before the change.
If a foreign trust becomes a DIFC trust, notwithstanding Article 9(2) the trustee may:
(4)

The terms of a DIFC trust may provide for a severable aspect of the trust (particularly the
(5)

trust.
The terms of a DIFC trust may provide for the law governing a severable aspect of the trust
(6)

Where the law governing a severable aspect of a DIFC trust is changed from the law of
(7)

invalid or unlawful any functions conferred on a trustee under the law of the DIFC.
Where the law governing a severable aspect of a DIFC trust is changed from the law of the
(8)

of the DIFC shall operate so as to render the trust void, invalid or unlawful or to render void,
invalid or unlawful any functions conferred on the trustee under the new law.
13.
Matters determined by governing law
Subject to Article 13(2), all matters arising in regard to a DIFC trust or in regard to any
(1)
disposition of property upon the trust thereof including questions as to:
the capacity of any settlor;
(a)
any aspect of the validity of the trust or disposition or the interpretation or effect
(b)
thereof;
the administration of the trust, whether the administration be conducted in the DIFC
(c)
or elsewhere, including questions as to the powers, obligations, liabilities and rights
of trustees and their appointment and removal; or
the existence and extent of powers, conferred or retained, including powers of
(d)
variation or revocation of the trust and powers of appointment, and the validity of
any exercise thereof,
shall be determined in accordance with the laws of the DIFC without reference to the laws
of any other jurisdiction with which the trust or disposition may be connected.
Subject to Articles 14, 15 and 16, Article 13(1) shall:
(2)
not validate any disposition of property which is neither owned by the settlor nor
(a)
is the subject of a power in that behalf vested in the settlor;
not validate any trust or disposition of immovable property situated in a jurisdiction
(b)
other than the DIFC in which such trust or disposition is invalid according to the
laws of such jurisdiction;
not validate any testamentary trust or disposition which is invalid according to the
(c)
laws of the testator's last domicile;

Limitations in foreign law
14.
Without limiting the generality of Article 13(1), no DIFC trust and no disposition of property
(1)
to be held in trust that is valid under the laws of the DIFC is void, voidable, liable to be set
aside or defective in any manner by reference to a foreign law; nor is the capacity of any
settlor in relation to the trust or disposition to be questioned; nor is the trustee or any
beneficiary or any other person to be subjected to any liability or deprived of any power or
right, by reason that:
the laws of any foreign jurisdiction prohibit or do not recognise the concept of a
(a)
trust;
the trust or disposition voids or defeats any rights, claims or interest conferred by
(b)
foreign law upon any person by reason of a personal relationship to the settlor or
any beneficiary or by way of heirship rights or contravenes any rule of foreign law
or any foreign, judicial or administrative order, arbitration award or action intended
to recognise, protect, enforce or give effect to any such rights, claims or interest; or
the foreign law or foreign judicial or administrative order or arbitration award
(c)
imposes any obligation or liability on the settlor, trustee, enforcer, protector,
beneficiary or any other party in relation to the trust or the trust property.
A transfer of property to a trust shall not be void, voidable or liable to be set aside by reason
(2)
of a settlor's bankruptcy, the liquidation of a Settlor, or any action or claims made against a
Settlor by any creditor, notwithstanding any foreign statute providing otherwise.
Notwithstanding Article 14(2), where the Court determines that:
(3)
at the time when the property was transferred to a trust, the settlor intended to
(a)
defraud a creditor; and
at the time such transfer took place the transfer of the property rendered the settlor
(b)
insolvent, or without sufficient property to satisfy a creditor's claim had the creditor
been successful in such claim,
then such transfer shall not be void or voidable or liable to be set aside and the DIFC trust
shall be liable to satisfy the creditor's claim and the trust's liability shall only be to the extent
of the interest the settlor had in the property prior to the transfer and of any accumulation to
the property transferred (if any) subsequent thereto.
Where a trust is liable to satisfy a creditor's claim in the manner provided for in Article
(4)
14(3), that creditor's rights to recovery shall be limited to the property referred to in that
Article, or to the proceeds of that property, to the exclusion of any claim, right or action
against any trustee or any other property of the trust.
(5)
In making claims under Article 14(3), the burden of proof shall rest with the creditor.

TRUST LAW
15.
Heirship rights
An heirship right conferred by foreign law in relation to the property of a living person shall not be
recognised as:
affecting the ownership of immovable property in the DIFC and movable property including
(a)
a Digital Asset wherever it is situated for the purposes of Article 13(2)(a) and (b) or for any
other purpose; or
(b)
constituting an obligation or liability for any purpose.
16.
Foreign judgments
A foreign judgment shall not be recognised or enforced or give rise to any estoppels insofar
(1)
as it is inconsistent with Articles 13, 14 and 15.
No proceedings for or in relation to the enforcement or recognition of a judgment obtained
(2)
in a jurisdiction other than the DIFC against:
a DIFC trust;
(a)
a settlor of a DIFC trust;
(b)
a trustee of a DIFC trust;
(c)
a protector of a DIFC trust;
(d)
a beneficiary of a DIFC trust;
(e)
a person appointed or instructed in accordance with the express or implied
(f)
provisions of an instrument or disposition to exercise a function or undertake any
act, matter or thing in connection with a DIFC trust; or
property of either a DIFC trust or of a trustee or a beneficiary thereof,
(g)
shall be entertained by the Court if that judgment is based upon the application of any law
inconsistent with the provisions of this Law.
16A.
Assertion of jurisdiction over trustee and protector
If any action is brought against a person in their capacity as a trustee of a DIFC trust in a
(1)
foreign court or tribunal having authority over the trustee, and such foreign court or tribunal
fails to dismiss such action and orders such trustee to take an action regarding the
administration of such trust or in respect of such trust's property, the trustee shall
immediately upon the action of such court or tribunal and without the further order of any
court, cease in all respects to be trustee of such trust and a successor trustee shall thereupon
succeed as trustee in accordance with the terms of the trust instrument or, if the trust
instrument does not provide for a successor trustee and the trust would otherwise be without
a trustee, the Court, upon the application of any beneficiary of such trust, shall appoint a
successor trustee upon such terms and conditions as it determines to be consistent with the
purposes of such trust and this Law.
Upon a trustee ceasing to be trustee under Article 16A(1), such trustee shall have no power
(2)
or authority other than to convey the trust property to the successor trustee named in the trust
instrument or appointed by the Court in accordance with this Article.
If any action brought against a person in their capacity as a protector of a DIFC trust in a
(3)
foreign court or tribunal having authority over the protector, and such foreign court or
tribunal fails to dismiss such action and orders such protector to take any action in regards
to the administration of such trust or in respect of such trust's property, the protector shall
6

immediately upon the action of such court or tribunal and without the further order of any

protector shall, immediately following its removal hereunder, give notice in writing to the

Place of administration
17.
Without precluding other means for establishing a close connection with the designated
(1)
jurisdiction, terms of a trust designating the place of administration are valid and conclusive
if:
a trustee's principal place of business is located in or a trustee is resident of the
(a)
designated jurisdiction; or
all or part of the administration occurs in the designated jurisdiction.
(b)
A trustee is under a continuing duty to administer the trust at a place appropriate to its
(2)
purposes, its administration, and the interests of the beneficiaries or in furtherance of its
purposes and in accordance with the terms of the trust.
Without precluding the right of the Court to order, approve, or disapprove a transfer in
(3)
furtherance of the duty prescribed in Article 17(2), the trustee may transfer the trust's place
of administration to another jurisdiction outside the DIFC.
In connection with a transfer of the trust's place of administration, the trustee may transfer
(4)
some or all of the trust property to a successor trustee designated in the terms of the trust.

TRUST LAW
PART 3: JUDICIAL AND NON JUDICIAL PROCEEDINGS
18.
Role of the Court in administration of trust
The Court may intervene in the administration of a trust to the extent its jurisdiction is
(1)
invoked by an interested person or as provided by Law.
A trust is not subject to continuing judicial supervision unless so ordered by the Court.
(2)
A judicial proceeding involving a trust may relate to any matter involving the trust's
(3)
administration, including a request for instructions and an action to declare rights.
19.
Jurisdiction of the Court
The Court has jurisdiction where:
the trust is a DIFC trust;
(a)
a trustee of a foreign trust is resident in the DIFC;
(b)
(c)
any trust property of a foreign trust is situated in the DIFC but only in respect of property so
situated; or
administration of any trust property of a foreign trust is carried out in the DIFC.
(d)
Application to and certain powers of the Court
20.
A trustee may make an application to the Court for direction, opinion or advice concerning
(1)
the manner in which he may or should act in connection with any matter concerning the trust
and the Court may make such order, if any, as it thinks fit.
The Court may if it thinks fit:
(2)
make an order concerning:
(a)
the execution or the administration of any trust;
(i)
the trustee of any trust, including an order relating to:
(ii)
the exercise of any power, discretion or duty of the trustee;
(A)
the appointment or removal of a trustee;
(B)
the remuneration of a trustee;
(C)
the submission of accounts;
(D)
the conduct of the trustee; and
(E)
payments, whether payments into Court or otherwise;
(F)
the vesting of trust property;
(iii)
a beneficiary or any person having a connection with the trust as the Court
(iv)
may determine; or
the appointment or removal of an enforcer in relation to any non-charitable
(v)
purposes of the trust;
make a declaration as to the validity or the enforceability of a trust;
(b)
8

Articles 31, 32 and Schedule 2: Interpretation
For the purposes of Articles 31 and 32 and Schedule 2:
"administration question" means any relief or question in respect of which an action, application
or other reference to the Court could be brought or made under this Law;
"dispute" includes a difference;
"power holder" means any person holding a power in relation to a trust (including any power of
appointment, consent, direction, revocation or variation, and any power to appoint or remove
trustee or power holders) and includes a person in the position of a protector or enforcer; and
"the parties in relation to the trust" means any trustee, beneficiary or power holder of or under the
trust, in their capacity as such.

TRUST LAW
31.
Arbitration of trust disputes
(1)
Where a trust instrument provides that any dispute or administration question arising
between any of the parties in relation to the trust shall be submitted to arbitration ("a trust
arbitration"), that provision shall, for all purposes under the Arbitration Law have effect as
between those parties as if it were an arbitration agreement and as if those parties were parties
to that agreement.
(2)
Where a trust instrument does not provide that any dispute or administration question arising
between any of the parties in relation to the trust shall be submitted to arbitration but the
parties to that dispute agree in writing to have it resolved by a trust arbitration, that agreement
shall, for all purposes under the Arbitration Law, have effect as between those parties as if it
were an arbitration agreement.
The Arbitration Law shall apply to a trust arbitration in accordance with the provisions of
(3)
Schedule 2.
The Court may make such orders in relation to an arbitration or possible arbitration which
(4)
supplement or vary the application of Schedule 2 as may in the opinion of the Court be
appropriate in the circumstances of the case.
Powers of the arbitral tribunal
32.
This Article shall apply except to the extent otherwise provided in the trust instrument.
(1)
The arbitral tribunal (hereinafter referred to as "the tribunal") may, in addition to all other
(2)
powers of the tribunal, at any stage in a trust arbitration, exercise all the powers of the Court
(whether arising by law (including this Law), under the inherent jurisdiction of the Court or
otherwise) in relation to the administration, execution or variation of a trust or the exercise
of any power arising under a trust.

TRUST LAW
PART 4: CREATION, VALIDITY AND MODIFICATION OF A DIFC TRUST
Creation of a trust
33.
A trust may be created by:
transfer of property to another person as trustee during the settlor's lifetime or by will or
(a)
other disposition taking effect upon the settlor's death;
the transfer of property from one (1) trust to another;
(b)
declaration by the owner of identifiable property that thereupon the owner will hold the
(c)
property as trustee;
exercise of a power of appointment in favour of a trustee; or
(d)
an instrument in writing including a will or codicil.
(e)
Requirements for creation
34.
A trust is created if:
(1)
the settlor has the capacity to create a trust;
(a)
the settlor indicates an intention to create the trust;
(b)
the trust either:
(c)
has a definite beneficiary;
(i)
is a charitable trust, as provided for in Article 38; or
(ii)
(iii)
is a non-charitable purpose trust, as provided for in Article 39;
the trustee holds or has vested in him or it, property for the benefit of a beneficiary
(d)
or for a purpose;
the trustee has duties to perform; and
(e)
the same person is not the sole trustee and sole beneficiary.
(f)
A beneficiary is definite if the beneficiary can be ascertained now or in the future.
(2)
A trust may have at the same time a definite beneficiary and a purpose.
(3)
35.
Trust purposes
A trust may only be created to the extent its purposes are sufficiently certain to allow the
(1)
trust to be carried out, lawful and not contrary to public policy in the DIFC.
A trust and its terms shall be for the benefit of its beneficiaries or in furtherance and support
(2)
of its purposes.
36.
Duration of a trust
A trust may continue indefinitely or terminate in accordance with this Law or with the terms
(1)
of the trust.
No rule against perpetuities or excessive accumulations shall apply to a trust or to any
(2)
advancement, appointment, payment or application of assets from a trust.
14

Validity and invalidity of a trust
Subject to Articles 37(2), (3) and (4), a trust shall be valid and enforceable in accordance
(1)
with its terms.
A trust shall be invalid to the extent that:
(2)
it purports to do anything which is contrary to DIFC law;
(a)
it is created for a purpose other than a charitable purpose in relation to which there
(b)
is no beneficiary, unless it complies with Article 39;
its creation was induced by fraud, duress, undue influence or misrepresentation;
(c)
the trust is immoral or contrary to public policy in the DIFC; or
(d)
(e)
the terms of the trust are so uncertain that its performance is rendered impossible.
Where a trust is created for two (2) or more purposes of which some are lawful and others
(3)
are unlawful:
if those purposes cannot be separated the trust shall be invalid; or
(a)
where those purposes can be separated the Court may declare that the trust is valid
(b)

A trust may be validly created which has beneficiaries, charitable purposes and purposes
(4)
which are not charitable, in which case Article 38 shall apply in relation to the charitable
purposes and Article 39 shall apply in relation to the purposes which are not charitable.
38.
Charitable trusts
A charitable trust may be created for the relief of poverty, the advancement of education or
(1)
religion, the promotion of health or art, the protection of the environment, or any other
purposes which are beneficial to the general public.
If the terms of a charitable trust do not indicate a particular charitable purpose or beneficiary
(2)
or a means by which a particular charitable purpose or beneficiary may be selected, the Court
may select one or more charitable purposes or beneficiaries in accordance with the settlor's
intention to the extent it can be ascertained.
Subject to Article 38(4) if a particular charitable purpose becomes unlawful, impracticable,
(3)
impossible to achieve, contrary to public policy in the DIFC, or obsolete in that, by reason
of changed circumstances, it fails to achieve the purpose of the trust:
the trust does not fail, in whole or in part;
(a)
the trust property does not revert to the settlor or the settlor's successors in interest;
(b)
and
(c)
the Court may apply cy-près to vary or terminate the trust by directing that the trust
property be applied or distributed, in whole or in part, to one (1) or more charitable
purposes or beneficiaries in accordance with the settlor's intention to the extent it
can be ascertained and otherwise as the Court may direct.

Variation and revocation of a trust
40.
A trust may expressly provide that:
(1)
(a)

(b)

(3)

(4)

(6)

all or such part of the trust property and accordingly shall have no responsibility to any other
person for acts or omissions occurring during that person's lifetime in respect of that
property.

(2)
affected by the failure or lapse.
Termination of a trust
43.
Without prejudice to the powers of the Court under this Law a trust terminates:
(1)
if the trust is revoked or expires pursuant to its terms;
(a)
if there is no beneficiary or person who can become a beneficiary in accordance
(b)
with the terms of the trust or if no purpose of the trust remains to be achieved;
if the purposes of the trust have become unlawful, or impossible to achieve; or
(c)
notwithstanding the terms of the trust, upon consent of all the beneficiaries in
(d)
existence who have been ascertained and none of whom is a minor or a person
under a legal disability.
The Court may terminate a trust:
(2)
because of circumstances not anticipated by the settlor, if termination will further
(a)
the purposes of the trust; or
if the value of the trust property is insufficient to justify the cost of administration.
(b)
An application to the Court under this Article may be made by a settlor, a trustee or a
(3)
beneficiary as the case may be.
Distribution of property
44.
Without prejudice to the powers of the Court under Article 44(3), on the termination of the
(1)
trust the trustee shall distribute the trust property to the persons entitled thereto within a
reasonable time and in accordance with the terms of the trust.
The trustee may retain sufficient assets or obtain satisfactory security to make reasonable
(2)
provision for liabilities, whether existing, future, contingent or otherwise, before distributing
the trust property under Article 44(1).
(3)
The Court may, on the termination of a trust or at any time thereafter, upon an application
made by a trustee or any beneficiary as the case may be:
require the trustee to distribute the trust property;
(a)

TRUST LAW
PART 5: THE BENEFICIARIES OF A DIFC TRUST.
45.
Beneficiaries of a trust
A beneficiary shall be:
(1)
identifiable by name; or
(a)
ascertainable by reference to:
(b)
a class or other identifying characteristic; or
(i)
a relationship to some person whether or not living at the time of the
(ii)
creation of the trust or at the time which under the terms of the trust is the
time by reference to which members of a class are to be determined.
The terms of a trust may provide for the addition of a person as a beneficiary or the exclusion
(2)
of a beneficiary from benefit.
A settlor, protector or a trustee of a trust may also be a beneficiary of a trust.
(3)
Disclaimer
46.
A beneficiary may disclaim his whole interest.
(1)
A disclaimer made under Article 46(1) shall be in writing and shall be irrevocable.
(2)
Subject to the terms of a trust, a beneficiary under a trust may disclaim part of his interest,
(3)
whether or not he has received some benefit from his interest.
A disclaimer made under Article 46(3) may, subject to the terms of the trust, be revocable
(4)
and it shall be exercisable in the manner and under the circumstances so expressed.
47.
Interest of beneficiary and dealings thereof
The interest of a beneficiary shall constitute movable property.
(1)
Subject to the terms of a trust, a beneficiary may, by instrument in writing, sell, charge,
(2)
transfer or otherwise deal with his interest in any manner.
48.
Termination of interest of beneficiary upon trust being challenged
The terms of a trust may provide that the interest of a beneficiary shall terminate (or may be
(1)
terminated in exercise of a power in that regard) upon:
the validity of the trust being challenged by the beneficiary, in whole or in part, in
(a)
any court within or outside the DIFC;
(b)
any action being taken by the beneficiary to assist, promote or encourage such a
challenge;
the beneficiary refusing to agree to have a dispute or difference in relation to the
(c)
affairs of the trust referred to arbitration; or
the beneficiary having acted in a manner detrimental to the welfare of the settlor or
(d)
the settlor's family or business, or the administration of the trust.
Unless otherwise provided in the trust instrument, no rule of law or equity giving relief
(2)
against forfeiture shall apply to a provision referred to in Article 48(1).
21

TRUST LAW
PART 6: PROTECTIVE TRUSTS AND CREDITORS' CLAIMS
49.
Protective trusts
The terms of a trust may make the interest of the beneficiary liable to termination.
(1)
Without prejudice to the generality of Article 49(1), the terms of a trust may make the interest
(2)
of a beneficiary in the income or capital of the trust property subject to:
a restriction on alienation or disposal; or
(a)
a diminution or termination in the event of the beneficiary becoming insolvent or
(b)
any of his property becoming liable to sequestration for the benefit of his creditors.
A trust under which the interest of a beneficiary is subject to restriction, diminution or
(3)
termination under Article 49(2) is a protective trust.
A provision in the terms of a trust requiring the interest of a beneficiary in trust property to
(4)
be held upon a protective trust shall be construed as a requirement that the interest of the
beneficiary be subject to restriction, diminution or termination as mentioned in Article 49(2).
Where any property is directed to be held as protective trust for the benefit of a beneficiary,
(5)
the trustee shall hold that property:
in trust to pay the income to the beneficiary until the income terminates in
(a)
accordance with the terms of the trust or a determining event occurs; and
if a determining event occurs while the interest of the beneficiary continues, in trust
(b)
to pay the interest to such of the following (and including more than one in such
shares) as the trustee in his or her absolute discretion shall determine:
the beneficiary;
(i)
(ii)
any spouse or child of the beneficiary; or
if there is no such spouse or child, persons who would be entitled to the
(iii)
estate of the beneficiary if the beneficiary had died intestate, without
regard to the domicile of such beneficiaries.
In Article 49(5) above, a "determining event" shall mean the occurrence of any event or any
(6)
act or omission on the part of the beneficiary (other than the giving of consent to an
advancement of trust property), which would result in the trust property becoming payable
to a person other than the beneficiary.
50.
Creditors' claim in relation to a discretionary trust
A Discretionary Interest in a DIFC trust is not a property interest or an enforceable right but
(1)
is a mere expectancy of a right, which a creditor of a beneficiary (including the settlor) may
not attach, garnish or otherwise reach.
In the case of a discretionary trust, whether or not such trust contains a protective provision,
(2)
a creditor of a beneficiary may not compel a distribution that is subject to the trustee's
discretion, even if:
the discretion is expressed in the form of a standard of distribution; or
(a)
the trustee has abused the discretion.
(b)
To the extent a trustee has not complied with a standard of distribution or has abused a
(3)
discretion:

a distribution may be ordered by the Court to satisfy a judgment or Court order
(a)
against the beneficiary for support or maintenance of the beneficiary's child, spouse
or former spouse; and
the Court shall direct the trustee to pay to the child, spouse, or former spouse such
(b)
amount as is equitable under the circumstances but not more than the amount the
trustee would have been required to distribute to or for the benefit of the beneficiary
had the trustee complied with the standard or not abused the discretion.
A creditor of a beneficiary (including the settlor) may not compel or force a protector to
(4)
exercise a power to direct a trustee to make a distribution to any beneficiary of a DIFC trust.
50A.
General power of appointment granted to a beneficiary
A creditor shall have no right against the interest of a beneficiary of a DIFC trust or against the
beneficiary or trustee of the trust with respect to such interest unless:
the beneficiary (other than the settlor) has a power to appoint all or part of the trust property
(a)
to the beneficiary, the beneficiary's estate, the beneficiary's creditors, or the creditors of the
beneficiary's estate by will or other instrument such that the appointment would take effect
only upon the beneficiary's death and the beneficiary actually exercises such power in favour
of the beneficiary, the beneficiary's creditors, the beneficiary's estate, or the creditors of the
beneficiary's estate but then only to the extent of such exercise; or
the beneficiary (other than the settlor) has a power, including a power of withdrawal, to
(b)
appoint all or part of the trust property to the beneficiary, the beneficiary's creditors, the
beneficiary's estate, or the creditors of the beneficiary's estate during the beneficiary's
lifetime and the beneficiary actually exercises such power in favour of the beneficiary, the
beneficiary's creditors, the beneficiary's estate, or the creditors of the beneficiary's estate
but then only to the extent of such exercise.
50B.
Right of revocation retained by settlor
A creditor shall have no right against the interest of a beneficiary of a DIFC trust who is the settlor
or against the settlor or trustee of the trust with respect to such interest unless the settlor has a power
to revoke the trust and appoint all or part of the trust property to the settlor, the settlor's creditors, the
settlor's estate, or the creditors of the settlor's estate during the settlor's lifetime and the settlor
actually exercises such power in favour of the settlor, the settlor's creditors, the settlor's estate, or the
creditors of the settlor's estate but then only to the extent of such exercise.

TRUST LAW
PART 7: OFFICE OF TRUSTEE
51.
Accepting or declining trusteeship
Except as otherwise provided in Article 51(3) a person designated as trustee accepts the
(1)
trusteeship:
by substantially complying with a method of acceptance provided in the terms of
(a)
the trust; or
if the terms of the trust do not provide a method or the method provided in the terms
(b)
is not expressly made exclusive, by accepting delivery of the trust property,
exercising powers or performing duties as trustee, or otherwise indicating
acceptance of the trusteeship.
A person designated as trustee who has not yet accepted the trusteeship may decline the
(2)
trusteeship. A designated trustee who does not accept the trusteeship within a reasonable
amount of time after knowing of the designation is deemed to have rejected the trusteeship.
A person designated as a trustee, without accepting the trusteeship, may without liability for
(3)
loss:
act to preserve the trust property if, within a reasonable time after acting, he sends
(a)
a written rejection of the trusteeship to the person (if any) with the power to appoint
a new trustee;
inspect or investigate trust property to determine potential liability under any law
(b)
(including the law of any jurisdiction in which the trust property is located) or for
any other purpose; or
apply to the Court for directions or advice.
(c)
A person who knowingly does any act or thing in relation to the trust property consistent
(4)
with the status of a trustee of that property shall be deemed to have accepted appointment as
a trustee, but he shall not be remunerated for acting in such capacity as provided in Article
56, unless the trustee appointed under the terms of the trust otherwise agrees.
52.
Vacancy in trusteeship and appointment of a new trustee
A vacancy in a trusteeship occurs if:
(1)
a person designated as trustee rejects the trusteeship;
(a)
a person designated as trustee cannot be identified or does not exist;
(b)
a trustee resigns;
(c)
a trustee is removed;
(d)
a trustee dies, becomes bankrupt, or, being a corporation, has a petition for its
(e)
winding up presented or an administrator appointed;
a guardian is appointed for an individual serving as trustee; or
(f)
a trustee is unable to freely exercise his rights or powers or to fulfil his duties or
(g)
obligations hereunder because of duress or undue influence brought to bear on such
person by any other person or persons.
25

Where the terms of a trust contain no provision for the appointment of a new trustee, the
(2)
trustee for the time being may appoint a new trustee or failing that the Court may appoint a

Subject to the terms of the trust, a trustee appointed under this Article shall have the same
(3)
powers, discretions and duties and may act as if he had been originally appointed a trustee.
A trustee having power to appoint a new trustee who fails to exercise such power may be
(4)
removed from office by the Court and the Court may appoint a new trustee.
If one (1) or more co-trustees remain in office, a vacancy in a trusteeship need not be filled.
(5)
A vacancy in trusteeship shall be filled if the trust has no remaining trustee.
(6)
Where there is no trustee a trust shall not fail on that account.
(7)
Resignation of trustee
53.
Subject to the terms of the trust, a trustee may resign his office:
(1)
by giving at least thirty (30) days' notice in writing to the person (if any) with the
(a)
power to appoint a new trustee, and all his co-trustees (if any). Such resignation
shall take effect upon termination of such thirty (30) day notice period or such
earlier date as shall be agreed between the trustee resigning and the person (if any)
with the power to appoint a new trustee, and his co-trustees (if any) provided that
if no replacement trustee has been appointed within thirty (30) days following the
giving of such notice and there are no continuing trustees, the trustee shall continue
in office and seek the advice of the Court;
with the approval of the Court; or
(b)
otherwise in accordance with the terms of the trust.
(c)
A resignation given in order to facilitate a breach of trust shall have no effect.
(2)
54.
Removal of trustee by Court or under the terms of a trust
The settlor, an enforcer, a co-trustee, a protector or a beneficiary may request the Court to
(1)
remove a trustee, or a trustee may be removed by the Court on its own initiative.
The Court may remove a trustee if:
(2)
the trustee has committed a breach of trust;
(a)
lack of cooperation among co-trustees substantially impairs the administration of
(b)
the trust;
because of unfitness, unwillingness, or persistent failure of the trustee to administer
(c)
the trust, the Court determines that removal of the trustee best serves the interests
of the beneficiaries; or
there has been a substantial change of circumstances or removal is requested by all
(d)
of the beneficiaries, the Court finds that removal of the trustee best serves the
interests of all the beneficiaries and is not inconsistent with a material purpose of
the trust, and a suitable co-trustee or successor trustee is available.
Pending a final decision on a request to remove a trustee, or in lieu of or in addition to
(3)
removing a trustee, the Court may order appropriate relief under Article 74.

A trustee who resigns or is removed shall proceed expeditiously to deliver the trust property
(1)
within the trustee's possession to the co-trustee, successor trustee or other person entitled to

the terms of the trust;

a trustee shall not be entitled to remuneration for his services.

(2)

(b)

(3)

57.

(1)

(2)
trust property:

TRUST LAW
PART 8: DUTIES AND POWERS OF TRUSTEES
CHAPTER 1 - DUTIES OF TRUSTEES
59.
Duty to administer a trust
Upon acceptance of a trusteeship, the trustee shall in the execution of his duties and in the
(1)
exercise of his powers and discretions:
act with due diligence as would a prudent person to the best of his ability and skill;
(a)
and
observe the utmost good faith,
(b)
in accordance with the terms and purposes of the trust and this Law.
A trustee shall administer the trust solely in the interest of the beneficiaries or in furtherance
(2)
or support of the purposes of the trust.
Duties of trustees
60.
Subject to the terms of the trust, a trustee shall so far as is reasonably practical preserve the
(1)
value of the trust property.
Except with the approval of the Court or as permitted by this Law or expressly provided by
(2)
the terms of the trust, a trustee shall not:
directly or indirectly profit from his trusteeship;
(a)
cause or permit any other person to profit directly or indirectly from such
(b)
trusteeship; or
on his own account enter into any transaction with the trustees or relating to the
(c)
trust property which may result in such profit.
Subject to Article 80, a sale, encumbrance, or other transaction involving the investment or
(3)
management of trust property entered into by the trustee for the trustee's own personal
account or which is otherwise affected by a conflict between the trustee's fiduciary and
personal interests is voidable by a beneficiary affected by the transaction unless:
the transaction was authorised by the terms of the trust;
(a)
the transaction was approved by the Court;
(b)
the beneficiary did not commence judicial proceedings within the time allowed by
(c)
Article 77; or
the beneficiary consented to the trustee's conduct or ratified the transaction.
(d)
A trustee shall keep accurate accounts and records of his trusteeship.
(4)

Subject to the terms of the trust, where there is more than one (1) beneficiary, or more than one (1)
purpose, the trustee shall act impartially and shall not execute the trust for the advantage of one (1)
at the expense of the other.
Cost of administration
63.
In administering a trust, the trustee may incur only costs that are reasonable in relation to the trust
property, the purposes of the trust and the skills of the trustee.

Enforcement and defence of claims
64.
A trustee may take reasonable steps to enforce claims of the trust and to defend claims against the
trust.
65.
Collecting trust property
A trustee shall take reasonable steps to compel a former trustee or other person to deliver trust
property to the trustee, and, subject to the terms of the trust, to redress a breach of trust known to the
trustee to have been committed by a former trustee.
66.
Duty to inform and report
Subject to the terms of a trust and any order of the Court, a trustee shall, on application in
(1)
writing by a beneficiary, disclose to the applicant all documents which relate to or form part
of the accounts of the trust.
A trustee shall not be required to disclose to any person any document which:
(2)
discloses his deliberations as to the manner in which he has exercised a power or
(a)
discretion or performed a duty conferred upon him;
discloses the reason for any particular exercise of such power or discretion or
(b)
performance of duty or the material upon which such reason shall or might have
been based; or
relates to the exercise or proposed exercise of such power or discretion or the
(c)

Notwithstanding the terms of the trust:

(a)

accountable to the beneficiaries or any of them; and
the Court may pursuant to such declaration extend or restrict the rights of all or any
(b)
beneficiaries to information regarding the trust or may make such other order as it
thinks fit.

(2)

A corporate trustee may:

72.
Subject to the terms of the trust, a trustee may combine two or more trusts into a single trust
(1)
or divide a trust into two (2) or more separate trusts, if the result does not impair rights of
any beneficiaries or adversely affect achievement of the purposes of the trusts.
Subject to the terms of the trust and Article 72(1), where a trustee divides a trust into two (2)
(2)
or more separate trusts, he shall have the power to:
appoint trustees for each of such trusts;
(a)

Subject to the terms of the trust, the trustee may take into consideration the difference in tax
(3)

in making applicable tax elections, and in making applications or distributions.
A separate trust created by severance must be treated as a separate trust for all purposes from
(4)
the date on which the severance is effective, including, but not limited to any and all issues
related to the liability of a trustee or the trust assets pursuant to a contract, in tort or otherwise.
In dividing a trust into two (2) or more separate trusts, a trustee shall accomplish the division
(5)
by severing the trusts on a fractional basis and funding the separate trusts either:
(a)
with a pro rata portion of each asset held by the undivided trust; or
on a non-pro rata basis based on either the fair market value of the assets on the
(b)
date of funding or in a manner that fairly reflects the net appreciation or

of funding.

PART 9: LIABILITY OF TRUSTEES AND RIGHTS OF PERSONS DEALING WITH A TRUSTEE
73.
Liability for breach of trust
Subject to this Law and to the terms of the trust, a trustee shall be liable for a breach of trust
(1)
committed by the trustee or in which the trustee has concurred.
A trustee who is liable for a breach of trust shall be liable for:
(2)
the loss or depreciation in value of the trust property resulting from such breach;
(a)
and
the profit, if any, which would have accrued to the trust property if there had been
(b)
no such breach.
Where there are two (2) or more breaches of trust, a trustee shall not set off a gain from one
(3)
(1) breach of trust against the loss resulting from another breach of trust.
A trustee shall not be liable for a breach of trust committed prior to his appointment, if such
(4)
breach of trust was committed by some other person.
A trustee shall not be liable for a breach of trust committed by a co-trustee unless:
(5)
he becomes aware or ought to have become aware of the commission of such breach
(a)
or of the intention of his co-trustee to commit a breach of trust; and
he actively conceals such breach or such intention or fails within a reasonable time
(b)
to take proper steps to protect or restore the trust property or prevent such breach.
A beneficiary may:
(6)
relieve a trustee of liability to him for a breach of trust; or
(a)
indemnify a trustee against liability for a breach of trust.
(b)
Article 73(6) shall not apply unless the beneficiary:
(7)
has legal capacity;
(a)
has full knowledge of all material facts; and
(b)
is not improperly induced by the trustee to take action under Article 73(6).
(c)
(8)
Where two (2) or more trustees are liable in respect of a breach of trust, they shall be liable
jointly and severally.
A trustee who becomes aware of a breach of trust under Article 73(4) shall take all reasonable
(9)
steps to have such breach remedied.
Nothing in terms of a trust shall relieve, release, or exonerate a trustee from liability for
(10)
breach of trust arising from his own fraud, willful misconduct or gross negligence.
74.
Remedies for breach of trust
To remedy a breach of trust that has occurred or may occur, the Court may:
compel the trustee to perform the trustee's duties;
(a)
restrain the trustee from committing a breach of trust;
(b)

75.
Damages in absence of a breach
Except as expressly provided in the terms of the trust, a trustee is accountable to the trust for
(1)
any profit made by the trustee arising from the administration of the trust, even absent a
breach of trust.
Except as expressly provided in the terms of the trust, absent a breach of trust, a trustee is
(2)

profit.
76.
Legal fees and costs
In a judicial proceeding involving the administration of a trust, the Court, as justice and equity may
require, may award costs and expenses, including reasonable lawyers' fees, to any party, to be paid
by another party or from the trust that is the subject of the controversy.
77.
Limitation of action against trustee
A person may not commence a proceeding against a trustee for breach of trust more than
(1)
three (3) years after the date such person or a representative of such person receives a report
from the trustee that adequately disclosed information that could form the basis for a
potential claim for breach of trust and informed such person or his representative of the time
allowed for commencing a proceeding.
(2)
A report adequately discloses the existence of a potential claim for breach of trust if it
provides sufficient information so that such person or representative knows of the potential
claim or should have inquired into its existence.
If Article 77(1) does not apply, a judicial proceeding by such person against a trustee for
(3)
breach of trust shall be commenced within seven (7) years of the first occurrence of:
the removal, resignation, or death of the trustee;
(a)
in relation to a claim by a beneficiary, the termination of the beneficiary's interest
(b)
in the trust; or
the termination of the trust.
(c)
No period of limitation shall apply to an action brought against a trustee:
(4)

Reliance on trust instrument
78.
Any person who acts in reasonable reliance on the terms of the trust as expressed in the trust

A term of a trust relieving a trustee of liability for breach of trust is unenforceable to the
(1)

relieves the trustee of liability for breach of trust committed in bad faith or with

any costs the trustee may incur:

(i)
in defending civil proceedings brought by a successor trustee or a
(ii)
beneficiary in which judgment is given against the trustee.
80.
Beneficiary's consent, release or ratification
Subject to Article 73(10), a trustee is not liable to a beneficiary for breach of trust if the
(1)
beneficiary consented to the conduct constituting the breach, released the trustee from
liability for the breach as provided in Article 73(6) or ratified the transaction constituting the
breach, unless:
the consent, release, or ratification of the beneficiary was induced by improper
(a)
conduct of the trustee; or
at the time of the consent, release, or ratification, the beneficiary did not know of
(b)
the beneficiary's rights or of the material facts relating to the breach.
Where a trustee commits a breach of trust at the instigation or at the request or with the
(2)
consent of a beneficiary, the Court may by order impound all or part of the interest of the
beneficiary by way of indemnity to the trustee or any person claiming through the trustee.

TRUST LAW
Limitation on personal liability of trustee
81.
Except as otherwise provided in the contract, a trustee is not personally liable on a contract
(1)
properly entered into in the trustee's fiduciary capacity in the course of administering the
trust, if the trustee in the contract disclosed the fiduciary capacity.
A trustee is personally liable for torts committed in the course of administering a trust, or for
(2)
obligations arising from ownership or control of trust property only if the trustee is
personally at fault.
(3)
A claim based on a contract entered into by a trustee in the trustee's fiduciary capacity or an
obligation arising from ownership or control of trust property, or on a tort committed in the
course of administering a trust, may be asserted in a judicial proceeding against the trustee
in the trustee's fiduciary capacity, whether or not the trustee is personally liable for the claim.
Protection of persons dealing with trustees
82.
A person other than a beneficiary who in good faith assists a trustee, or who in good faith
(1)
and for value deals with a trustee, without knowledge that the trustee is exceeding or
improperly exercising the trustee's powers is protected from liability as if the trustee properly
exercised the power.
A person other than a beneficiary who in good faith deals with a trustee is not required to
(2)
inquire into the extent of the trustee's powers or the propriety of their exercise.
(3)
A person who in good faith delivers assets to a trustee need not ensure their proper
application.
A person other than a beneficiary who in good faith assists a former trustee or who in good
(4)
faith and for value deals with a former trustee, without knowledge that the trusteeship has
terminated is protected from liability as if the former trustee were still a trustee.
Comparable protective provisions of other DIFC laws relating to commercial transactions or
(5)
transfer of securities by fiduciaries prevail over the protection provided by this Article.
83.
Trustees of more than one trust
A trustee acting as such for more than one (1) trust shall not, in the absence of fraud or bad faith, be
affected by notice of matter in relation to a particular trust if the trustee has obtained notice of it
merely by reason of acting or having acted for the business of another trust.
41

any or all of such powers) shall not:
invalidate the trust;
(a)
(b)
prevent the trust taking effect according to its terms; or
cause any or all of the trust property, or the interests or powers reserved, to be

TRUST LAW
85.
Incapacitation of protector or settlor
If the settlor becomes incapacitated then the rights or powers (if any) reserved to him shall
(1)
during his incapacitation be exercisable by the protector (if appointed) or by any other
person designated by the trust instrument or appointed for that purpose by the Court
declaring or confirming him to be incapacitated.
If the protector becomes incapacitated then the rights or powers (if any) reserved to
(2)
him shall during his incapacitation be exercisable by the person designated by the trust
instrument or appointed for that purpose by the Court declaring or confirming him to
be incapacitated.
For the purposes of this Article:
(3)
a person shall be deemed to be incapacitated if declared to be of unsound mind
(a)
by a court of competent jurisdiction or if declared by two (2) qualified examining
physicians to be of unsound mind or physically impaired so as to be unable to act
responsibly prudently or effectively and shall be deemed to remain incapacitated
until declared otherwise by such court or such physicians; and
a corporation shall be deemed to be incapacitated if it has been dissolved, or if it
(b)
is under administration or a petition for its winding up has been presented in any
Court and has not been finally determined by the Court.
The trustee may declare in writing a person to be incapacitated during any period in
(4)
which the trustee is of the opinion that such person is unable freely to exercise his
rights or powers or to fulfil his duties or obligations hereunder because of duress or
undue influence brought to bear on such person by any other person or persons but the
trustee shall not be under any duty to make any inquiries as to whether any person is
suffering from duress or undue influence and in any case the trustee shall not be under
any duty to make any declaration as aforementioned.
85A.
Duress
Subject to Article 85A(2), to the extent any person is granted the power under the terms
(1)
of a trust to demand or request any act on the part of a settlor, beneficiary, trustee,
protector, or other person, or has the authority to approve, veto, or compel any action
or exercise any power which affects or will affect a trust or any interest therein, each
such person is directed, to the extent such person would not be subject to personal
liability or personal exposure:
to accept or recognise only demands or requests, or the effects of any approval,
(a)
veto, or compelled action or the exercise of any power, which are given by or are
the result of persons acting of their own free will and not under compulsion or
pursuant to any legal process, directive, order, or like decree of any court (other
than the Court), administrative body, or other tribunal or like authority; and
to ignore any demands or requests, or the effects of any approval, veto, or
(b)
compelled action or the exercise of any power, where the person attempting to
demand, request, approve, veto, compel the act, or exercise the power is not a
person either appointed or so authorised pursuant to the terms of such trust.
Article 85A(1) shall not apply to any decision, directive, notice or order issued by the
(2)
Registrar in relation to a trust, pursuant to the powers of the Registrar under the
Operating Law.
44

TRUST LAW
PART 11: PROVISIONS APPLICABLE TO A FOREIGN TRUST
86.
Enforceability of a foreign trust
Subject to Article 86(2), a foreign trust shall be regarded as being governed by, and shall
(1)
be interpreted in accordance with, its governing law.
A foreign trust shall be unenforceable in the DIFC:
(2)
to the extent that it purports:
(a)
(i)
to do anything which is contrary to DIFC law; or
to confer any right or power or impose any obligation the exercise of
(ii)
which is contrary to DIFC law; or
to the extent that the Court declares that the trust is immoral or contrary to public
(b)
policy in the DIFC,
but otherwise shall be enforceable in accordance with its terms and its governing law.
45

TRUST LAW
SCHEDULE 1
INTERPRETATION
1.
Rules of interpretation
In the Law, a reference to:
(1)
a statutory provision includes a reference to the statutory provision as amended or re-enacted from
(a)
time to time;
a "person" includes any natural person, body corporate or body unincorporate, including a
(b)
company, partnership, unincorporated association, government or state;
an obligation to publish or cause to be published a particular document shall, unless expressly
(c)
provided otherwise in the Law, includes publishing or causing to be published in printed or
electronic form;
a "day" means a calendar day, unless expressly stated otherwise. If an obligation falls on a calendar
(d)
day which is either a Saturday, Sunday or an official public holiday, the obligation shall take place
on the next calendar day which is a business day;
a "business day" means a calendar day, excluding Saturdays, Sundays and official public holidays;
(e)
a "week" shall mean a calendar week or seven (7) days, whichever is applicable in the
(f)
circumstances;
a "month" shall mean a period of thirty (30) days;
(g)
a "year" shall mean a period of three hundred and sixty five (365) days and a "calendar year" shall
(h)
mean a year of the Gregorian calendar;
a reference to the masculine gender includes the feminine and vice versa;
(i)
the singular shall include the plural and vice versa; and
(j)
"dollar" or "$" is a reference to United States Dollars unless the contrary intention appears.
(k)
The headings in the Law shall not affect its interpretation.
(2)
A reference in this Law to a Part, Chapter, Article or Schedule by number only, and without further
(3)
identification, is a reference to the Part, Chapter, Article or Schedule of that number in this Law.
A reference in an Article or other division of this Law to an Article by number or letter only, and without
(4)
further identification, is a reference to the Article of that number or letter contained in the Article or other
division of this Law in which that reference occurs.
Unless the context otherwise requires, where this Law refers to an enactment, the reference is to that
(5)
enactment as amended from time to time, and includes a reference to that enactment as extended or applied
by or under another enactment, including any other provision of that enactment.
References in this Law to writing, filing, instrument or certificate include any mode of communication that
(6)
preserves a record of the information contained therein and is capable of being reproduced in tangible form,
including electronic means.
46

Legislation in the DIFC
2.
References to legislation and Guidance in the Law shall be construed in accordance with the following
provisions:
Federal Law is law made by the federal government of the United Arab Emirates;
(a)
Dubai Law is law made by the Ruler, as applicable in the Emirate of Dubai;
(b)
DIFC law is law made by the Ruler (including, by way of example, the Law), as applicable in the
(c)
DIFC;
this Law is the Trust Law, DIFC Law No.4 of 2018 made by the Ruler;
(d)
the Regulations are Legislation made by the Board of Directors of the DIFCA under this law and
(e)
are binding in nature;
the Enactment Notice is the enactment notice pursuant to which this Law is brought into force; and
(f)
Guidance is indicative and non-binding and may comprise (i) guidance made and issued by the
(g)
DIFCA under this Law or the Regulations; and (ii) any standard or code of practice issued by the
Board of Directors of the DIFCA which has not been incorporated into the Regulations.
3.

TRUST LAW
SCHEDULE 2
APPLICATION OF THE ARBITRATION LAW
The Arbitration Law shall apply and be construed with respect to a trust arbitration, as stated hereunder.
1.
2.
In the Arbitration Law, "dispute" includes an administration question.
Article 12(1) of the Arbitration Law shall apply as if it read -
3.
"The settlor of a trust shall be free to determine (by provision in the trust instrument) how, in relation to a
trust, disputes are resolved, subject only to such safeguards as are necessary in the public interest."
Where in the Arbitration Law reference is made to a matter agreed between the parties to an arbitration
4.
agreement (including a matter which may be authorised, chosen, conferred, designated, nominated or vested
by the parties) that matter shall (except where no effective provision is made) be determined as provided in
the trust instrument.
5.
Neither Article 12 of the Arbitration Law nor any rule of law or construction treating an arbitration
agreement separate to any agreement of which it is a part shall apply in relation to a trust arbitration.
The term "action" in Article 13 of the Arbitration Law includes an application or other reference to the
6.
Court concerning an administration question which the trust instrument requires to be submitted to
arbitration and a stay of that application or other reference may be sought by any of the parties in
relation to the trust, whether or not a party to that application or other reference.
7.
In any application or other reference to the Court referred to in paragraph 6, the Court may stay the
proceedings on its own volition unless all parties in relation to the trust affected by the application are
before it or are represented by persons before it.
8.
Article 38 of the Arbitration Law shall apply as if it included the following Articles:
"(6) Where a person is or has been a party to a trust arbitration in the capacity of trustee he shall, unless
the tribunal otherwise orders, be entitled to the costs of the arbitration, in so far as they are not recovered
from or paid by any other person, out of the fund held by the trustee; and the tribunal may otherwise order
only on the ground that the trustee has in substance acted for his own benefit rather than for the benefit of
the trust.
(7) Where a person is or has been a party to a trust arbitration in the capacity of trustee and is entitled to
be paid his costs out of the fund held by the trustee any doubt as to whether costs were reasonably incurred
shall be resolved in favour of the trustee. Costs shall be presumed to have been unreasonably incurred if
they were incurred contrary to the duty of the trustee."
9.
For the purposes of enforcing an arbitral award under Article 41(2)(a)(iii) of the Arbitration Law, the
term "dispute" includes an administration question.
50

### 4e387152960c1029b3711cacb05b287b13c977bc61f2558059a62b7b427a62eb-p6-t1

- page_span: [6]
- header_signature: []

| 11. | Governing law |
| --- | --- |
| (1) | The meaning and effect of the terms of a trust are: |
| (a) | determined by the law of the jurisdiction expressed by the terms of the trust as the |
| (b) | to be implied from the terms of the trust; or failing which |
| (c) | to be determined by the law with which the trust at the time it was created had the |
| (2) | The references in Article 11(1)(a) and (b) to "failing which" include references to cases: |
| (a) | where no law is expressed or implied under Article 11(1)(a) or (b); and |
| (b) | where a law is so expressed or implied, but that law does not provide for trusts or |
| (3) | In ascertaining, for the purpose of Article 11(1)(c), the law with which a trust had the closest |
| (a) | the place of administration of the trust designated by the settlor; |
| (b) | the situs of the assets of the trust; |
| (c) | the place of residence or business of the trustee; and |
| (d) | the objects of the trust and the places where they are to be fulfilled. |
| (4) | A settlor may, whether or not he is resident in the DIFC, expressly declare in the trust |
| (5) | A term of the trust expressly declaring that the laws of the DIFC shall govern the trust is |
| 12. | Provision for change of governing law |
| (1) | Where a term of a trust so provides, the governing law may be changed to or from the law |

| 18. | Role of the Court in administration of trust |
| --- | --- |
| (1) | The Court may intervene in the administration of a trust to the extent its jurisdiction is |
| (2) | A trust is not subject to continuing judicial supervision unless so ordered by the Court. |
| (3) | A judicial proceeding involving a trust may relate to any matter involving the trust's |
| 19. | Jurisdiction of the Court |
| (a) | the trust is a DIFC trust; |
| (b) | a trustee of a foreign trust is resident in the DIFC; |
| (c) | any trust property of a foreign trust is situated in the DIFC but only in respect of property so |
| (d) | administration of any trust property of a foreign trust is carried out in the DIFC. |
| 20. | Application to and certain powers of the Court |
| (1) | A trustee may make an application to the Court for direction, opinion or advice concerning |
| (2) | The Court may if it thinks fit: |
| (a) | make an order concerning: |
| (i) | the execution or the administration of any trust; |
| (ii) | the trustee of any trust, including an order relating to: |
| (A) | the exercise of any power, discretion or duty of the trustee; |
| (B) | the appointment or removal of a trustee; |
| (C) | the remuneration of a trustee; |
| (D) | the submission of accounts; |
| (E) | the conduct of the trustee; and |
| (F) | payments, whether payments into Court or otherwise; |
| (iii) | the vesting of trust property; |
| (iv) | a beneficiary or any person having a connection with the trust as the Court |
| (v) | the appointment or removal of an enforcer in relation to any non-charitable |
| (b) | make a declaration as to the validity or the enforceability of a trust; |

| 33. | Creation of a trust |
| --- | --- |
| (a) | transfer of property to another person as trustee during the settlor's lifetime or by will or |
| (b) | the transfer of property from one (1) trust to another; |
| (c) | declaration by the owner of identifiable property that thereupon the owner will hold the |
| (d) | exercise of a power of appointment in favour of a trustee; or |
| (e) | an instrument in writing including a will or codicil. |
| 34. | Requirements for creation |
| (1) | A trust is created if: |
| (a) | the settlor has the capacity to create a trust; |
| (b) | the settlor indicates an intention to create the trust; |
| (c) | the trust either: |
| (i) | has a definite beneficiary; |
| (ii) | is a charitable trust, as provided for in Article 38; or |
| (iii) | is a non-charitable purpose trust, as provided for in Article 39; |
| (d) | the trustee holds or has vested in him or it, property for the benefit of a beneficiary |
| (e) | the trustee has duties to perform; and |
| (f) | the same person is not the sole trustee and sole beneficiary. |
| (2) | A beneficiary is definite if the beneficiary can be ascertained now or in the future. |
| (3) | A trust may have at the same time a definite beneficiary and a purpose. |
| 35. | Trust purposes |
| (1) | A trust may only be created to the extent its purposes are sufficiently certain to allow the |
| (2) | A trust and its terms shall be for the benefit of its beneficiaries or in furtherance and support |
| 36. | Duration of a trust |
| (1) | A trust may continue indefinitely or terminate in accordance with this Law or with the terms |
| (2) | No rule against perpetuities or excessive accumulations shall apply to a trust or to any |

| 37. | Validity and invalidity of a trust |
| (1) | Subject to Articles 37(2), (3) and (4), a trust shall be valid and enforceable in accordance |
| (2) | A trust shall be invalid to the extent that: |
| (a) | it purports to do anything which is contrary to DIFC law; |
| (b) | it is created for a purpose other than a charitable purpose in relation to which there |
| (c) | its creation was induced by fraud, duress, undue influence or misrepresentation; |
| (d) | the trust is immoral or contrary to public policy in the DIFC; or |
| (e) | the terms of the trust are so uncertain that its performance is rendered impossible. |
| (3) | Where a trust is created for two (2) or more purposes of which some are lawful and others |
| (a) | if those purposes cannot be separated the trust shall be invalid; or |
| (b) | where those purposes can be separated the Court may declare that the trust is valid |
| (4) | A trust may be validly created which has beneficiaries, charitable purposes and purposes |
| 38. | Charitable trusts |
| (1) | A charitable trust may be created for the relief of poverty, the advancement of education or |
| (2) | If the terms of a charitable trust do not indicate a particular charitable purpose or beneficiary |
| (3) | Subject to Article 38(4) if a particular charitable purpose becomes unlawful, impracticable, |
| (a) | the trust does not fail, in whole or in part; |
| (b) | the trust property does not revert to the settlor or the settlor's successors in interest; |
| (c) | the Court may apply cy-près to vary or terminate the trust by directing that the trust |

| 45. | Beneficiaries of a trust |
| --- | --- |
| (1) | A beneficiary shall be: |
| (a) | identifiable by name; or |
| (b) | ascertainable by reference to: |
| (i) | a class or other identifying characteristic; or |
| (ii) | a relationship to some person whether or not living at the time of the |
| (2) | The terms of a trust may provide for the addition of a person as a beneficiary or the exclusion |
| (3) | A settlor, protector or a trustee of a trust may also be a beneficiary of a trust. |
| 46. | Disclaimer |
| (1) | A beneficiary may disclaim his whole interest. |
| (2) | A disclaimer made under Article 46(1) shall be in writing and shall be irrevocable. |
| (3) | Subject to the terms of a trust, a beneficiary under a trust may disclaim part of his interest, |
| (4) | A disclaimer made under Article 46(3) may, subject to the terms of the trust, be revocable |
| 47. | Interest of beneficiary and dealings thereof |
| (1) | The interest of a beneficiary shall constitute movable property. |
| (2) | Subject to the terms of a trust, a beneficiary may, by instrument in writing, sell, charge, |
| 48. | Termination of interest of beneficiary upon trust being challenged |
| (1) | The terms of a trust may provide that the interest of a beneficiary shall terminate (or may be |
| (a) | the validity of the trust being challenged by the beneficiary, in whole or in part, in |
| (b) | any action being taken by the beneficiary to assist, promote or encourage such a |
| (c) | the beneficiary refusing to agree to have a dispute or difference in relation to the |
| (d) | the beneficiary having acted in a manner detrimental to the welfare of the settlor or |
| (2) | Unless otherwise provided in the trust instrument, no rule of law or equity giving relief |

### 4e387152960c1029b3711cacb05b287b13c977bc61f2558059a62b7b427a62eb-p41-t1

- page_span: [41]
- header_signature: []

| 73. | Liability for breach of trust |
| --- | --- |
| (1) | Subject to this Law and to the terms of the trust, a trustee shall be liable for a breach of trust |
| (2) | A trustee who is liable for a breach of trust shall be liable for: |
| (a) | the loss or depreciation in value of the trust property resulting from such breach; |
| (b) | the profit, if any, which would have accrued to the trust property if there had been |
| (3) | Where there are two (2) or more breaches of trust, a trustee shall not set off a gain from one |
| (4) | A trustee shall not be liable for a breach of trust committed prior to his appointment, if such |
| (5) | A trustee shall not be liable for a breach of trust committed by a co-trustee unless: |
| (a) | he becomes aware or ought to have become aware of the commission of such breach |
| (b) | he actively conceals such breach or such intention or fails within a reasonable time |
| (6) | A beneficiary may: |

| express trust | is a trust created with the settlor's express intent declared in writing or a written |

| foreign trust | is a trust whose governing law is the law of a jurisdiction other than DIFC, including |

| Hague Convention | the Convention on the law applicable to trusts and on their recognition, done at The |
| heirship right | any right, claim or interest in, against or to property of a person arising, accruing or |
| Income | includes rents and profits. |
